To solve this problem, we need to identify the paths between the two black nodes (N and G) and determine the minimum number of edges to remove to disconnect them. Let's start by identifying the paths.

1. **Identify the two black nodes:**
   - Node N
   - Node G

2. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of N and G:**
   - Neighbors of N: Y, C, B
   - Neighbors of G: K, H, X, C, F

3. **Find the paths between N and G:**
   - Path 1: N - Y - C - H - G
   - Path 2: N - Y - C - G
   - Path 3: N - Y - C - F - G
   - Path 4: N - B - E - F - G
   - Path 5: N - B - E - F - K - G
   - Path 6: N - B - E - L - C - F - G
   - Path 7: N - B - E - L - C - H - G
   - Path 8: N - B - E - L - C - G

4. **Evaluate the paths:**
   - We need to remove edges that interrupt all paths between N and G.
   - Removing the edge (C, G) will disconnect the paths involving C (paths 2, 3, and 8).
   - Removing the edge (E, F) will disconnect the paths involving E (paths 4, 5, and 6).
   - Removing the edge (B, N) will disconnect all remaining paths involving B (paths 4, 5, 6, and 7).
   - Removing the edge (H, C) will ensure that all paths are disconnected.

5. **Check the minimal number of edges to remove:**
   - Removing (C, G), (E, F), and (B, N) is sufficient to disconnect all paths.

Thus, the minimum number of edges that must be removed is 3.

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(C, G), (E, F), (B, N)].

--- Model Response ---
To solve this problem, we need to identify all paths between the two black nodes (B and M) and determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to ensure these nodes are completely disconnected.

1. **Identify the two black nodes:**
   - The black nodes are B and M.

2. **Identify immediate neighboring nodes:**
   - B is connected to H and C.
   - M is connected to E and J.

3. **Find all intermediate nodes and paths:**
   - B -> H -> E -> M
   - B -> H -> G -> F -> E -> M
   - B -> H -> G -> X -> K -> F -> E -> M
   - B -> H -> I -> J -> M
   - B -> H -> I -> P -> J -> M
   - B -> C -> N -> O -> Y -> P -> J -> M
   - B -> C -> N -> O -> Y -> H -> I -> J -> M

4. **Identify bottleneck edges:**
   - To completely disconnect B and M, we need to remove enough edges to eliminate all possible paths.
   - Observing the paths, we see that:
     - Removing edge H-E will disconnect the path B -> H -> E -> M.
     - Removing edge H-I will disconnect the paths B -> H -> I -> J -> M and B -> H -> I -> P -> J -> M.
     - Removing edge C-N will disconnect the path B -> C -> N -> O -> Y -> P -> J -> M.
     - However, removing just these three edges doesn't cover all paths. Specifically, the path B -> H -> G -> F -> E -> M and B -> C -> N -> O -> Y -> H -> I -> J -> M remain.

5. **Evaluate the minimal cut set:**
   - To ensure all paths are covered, we need to consider additional edges.
   - Removing edges H-G, G-F, and G-X will cover the path B -> H -> G -> F -> E -> M.
   - Removing edge C-N and N-O will cover the path B -> C -> N -> O -> Y -> H -> I -> J -> M.

   By removing the edges (H, E), (H, I), (C, N), (H, G), and (G, F), we ensure that all paths between B and M are disconnected.

**Output Format:**

Edges to remove: 5, Cut Set: [(H, E), (H, I), (C, N), (H, G), (G, F)].
--- Ground Truth ---
